I mean, I’m still getting by, but I’ve missed a couple of deadlines for my classes. I even had to back out of helping build the set for the theater department because I just couldn’t keep everything straight in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a loss of interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or time with friends?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I still like doing my usual stuff... hanging out with my friends, working in the shop. It's not that I don't care about them or don't want to do them anymore. I just... It's really just... when I'm at the house and things are loud, or when I'm stressing about how much I have to get done. \n\nWhen I'm actually at the shop or out of the house, my mood is usually fine. I'm just tired, mostly. But no, I'm not feeling down all day long.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling overly worried or overwhelmed about things you need to get done, like schoolwork or responsibilities at home?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm overly worried or anything. I mean, sure, I get a little stressed when I have a lot on my plate, but it's not like I'm sitting around panicking about it. I just try to get it done. It's more just the noise and the lack of sleep at the house that's getting to me, not really the actual work I have to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your stress and low mood are directly related to your sister and her baby moving back home, or are they more about other things going on in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely about them moving back in. That's actually why I came in. Ever since my sister and the baby got here, I've just been... more on edge, I guess. And down than usual. I find myself snapping over little stuff at the house, which isn't like me. \n\nAnd then, because of all the noise and everything, I show up to my classes or the shop already completely drained. I'm still getting by, mostly, but I've missed a couple of deadlines lately. I even had to back out of helping build the set for the theater department because I just couldn't keep everything straight in my head. So yeah, it all really started when they moved back.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt down or stressed even before your sister and her baby moved back home, or has this all been clearly tied to that situation?\n\nPatient: No, I was actually doing fine before they moved in. Like I said, it really is just tied to them being here. Everything was normal, and then... I'd say within a couple of weeks of the house getting so crowded and loud, that's when the trouble started.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting two weeks or more where you felt down nearly every day and stopped enjoying most activities, even before your sister and the baby moved back in?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I've never had a time where I felt down like that for weeks on end, or stopped doing things I like. Before they moved in, everything was pretty normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days when even the things you usually enjoy, like hanging out with friends or working in the shop, didn’t feel good or rewarding to you at all?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I still like hanging out with my friends, and I still like working in the shop. It's just that I'm so tired and on edge from the house being a mess that I don't always have the energy for it. But when I do go, I still enjoy it. It's not like I've lost interest in everything, I'm just... exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your low mood or stress continuing even when things are calm at home or when you're away from the house for a while, or does it only come up when you're dealing with the noise and chaos there?\n\nPatient: Well, the thing is, the situation hasn't really ended yet. My sister and her newborn are still living with us. So... even if I step out of the house for a bit or things are quiet for a minute, it's all still tied to that same change at home. It's just kind of always there because I know I have to go back to it.\n\nDoctor: Even when you're away from home, like when you're at the shop or with friends, do you ever find yourself thinking about the situation at home and feeling stressed or down about it, or does it only bother you while you're actually at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely still think about it when I'm not there. Like, if I'm at the shop or hanging out, it's not like I forget they're living with us. It's always in the back of my mind.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I find myself snapping over little stuff at the house, which isn't like me. \n\nAnd then, because of all the noise and everything, I show up to my classes or the shop already completely drained. I'm still getting by, mostly, but I've missed a couple of deadlines lately. I even had to back out of helping build the set for the theater department because I just couldn't keep everything straight in my head. So yeah, it all really started when they moved back.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt down or stressed even before your sister and her baby moved back home, or has this all been clearly tied to that situation?\n\nPatient: No, I was actually doing fine before they moved in. Like I said, it really is just tied to them being here. Everything was normal, and then... I'd say within a couple of weeks of the house getting so crowded and loud, that's when the trouble started.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting two weeks or more where you felt down nearly every day and stopped enjoying most activities, even before your sister and the baby moved back in?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I've never had a time where I felt down like that for weeks on end, or stopped doing things I like. Before they moved in, everything was pretty normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days when even the things you usually enjoy, like hanging out with friends or working in the shop, didn’t feel good or rewarding to you at all?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I still like hanging out with my friends, and I still like working in the shop. It's just that I'm so tired and on edge from the house being a mess that I don't always have the energy for it. But when I do go, I still enjoy it. It's not like I've lost interest in everything, I'm just... exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your low mood or stress continuing even when things are calm at home or when you're away from the house for a while, or does it only come up when you're dealing with the noise and chaos there?\n\nPatient: Well, the thing is, the situation hasn't really ended yet. My sister and her newborn are still living with us. So... even if I step out of the house for a bit or things are quiet for a minute, it's all still tied to that same change at home. It's just kind of always there because I know I have to go back to it.\n\nDoctor: Even when you're away from home, like when you're at the shop or with friends, do you ever find yourself thinking about the situation at home and feeling stressed or down about it, or does it only bother you while you're actually at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely still think about it when I'm not there. Like, if I'm at the shop or hanging out, it's not like I forget they're living with us. My sister and her newborn are still living with us. So... even if I step out of the house for a bit or things are quiet for a minute, it's all still tied to that same change at home. I'd say within a couple of weeks of the house getting so crowded and loud, that's when the trouble started.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting two weeks or more where you felt down nearly every day and stopped enjoying most activities, even before your sister and the baby moved back in?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I've never had a time where I felt down like that for weeks on end, or stopped doing things I like. Before they moved in, everything was pretty normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days when even the things you usually enjoy, like hanging out with friends or working in the shop, didn’t feel good or rewarding to you at all?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I still like hanging out with my friends, and I still like working in the shop. It's just that I'm so tired and on edge from the house being a mess that I don't always have the energy for it. But when I do go, I still enjoy it. It's not like I've lost interest in everything, I'm just... exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your low mood or stress continuing even when things are calm at home or when you're away from the house for a while, or does it only come up when you're dealing with the noise and chaos there?\n\nPatient: Well, the thing is, the situation hasn't really ended yet. My sister and her newborn are still living with us. So... even if I step out of the house for a bit or things are quiet for a minute, it's all still tied to that same change at home. It's just kind of always there because I know I have to go back to it.\n\nDoctor: Even when you're away from home, like when you're at the shop or with friends, do you ever find yourself thinking about the situation at home and feeling stressed or down about it, or does it only bother you while you're actually at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely still think about it when I'm not there. Like, if I'm at the shop or hanging out, it's not like I forget they're living with us. I'll be having a good time, but then I'll remember I have to go back to the noise and the mess, and it just kind of brings me down. It's always in the back of my mind.\n\nDoctor: How long has it been since your sister and her baby moved back home, and would you say your stress and low mood have improved at all during that time?\n\nPatient: It's been about a month now. Yeah, right around a month since my sister and the baby moved back into the house. And... no, I wouldn't really say it's improved at all. It's pretty much just been this same level of stress the whole time they've been here.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that if your sister and the baby were to move out and the situation at home returned to how it was before, your mood would also return to normal, or do you feel like something else is going on that would still be affecting you?\n\nPatient: I think if they moved out, things would probably go back to normal for me. Like I said before, the situation hasn't really ended yet since they're still living with us. So, all this stress and feeling down... it's all just tied directly to that change at home. I don't really think there's anything else going on. It's just the noise and the whole situation with them being there.\n\nDoctor: Even when you're away from home and not actively thinking about the situation with your sister and the baby, do you ever notice a low mood just coming up on its own, or does your mood stay about the same until you start thinking about going back home?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed it just coming up out of nowhere. Like, if I'm out and I'm distracted, I feel fine. My mood is normal. It's only when I actually think about the house, or when I remember that I have to head back there, that I start feeling down again. So, yeah, it doesn't just happen on its own.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed a low mood or lack of energy even during times when you're not thinking about the situation at home and are fully distracted, like when you're focused on a project at the shop or really into a movie with friends?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said, if I'm busy at the shop or hanging out, I feel pretty normal. It doesn't just hit me out of the blue if I'm not thinking about the house. It's really just tied to all that stuff going on at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any symptoms like changes in appetite, trouble sleeping, or feelings of worthlessness related to this situation, or is it mainly just feeling tired and stressed about the noise and chaos at home?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had any of that. My appetite is fine, and I don't feel worthless or anything like that. My sleep is a little disrupted sometimes just because of the baby crying, but it's not like I'm lying awake for no reason. It really is mostly just feeling stressed out and kind of drained from all the chaos and noise at the house.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood and stress have started to affect your ability to function in important areas of your life, like school, work, or relationships, beyond just missing a few deadlines or stepping back from helping with the theater set?\n\nPatient: No, not really beyond what I already mentioned. I mean, I'm still going to all my classes and showing up for work at the shop. It's just... I've turned in a couple of assignments late, which isn't usually like me, and I did have to drop out of building the set for the community theater. I just couldn't keep up with everything with how things are at home. But other than that, I'm still functioning okay, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your reaction to this situation has been more intense or longer-lasting than what you’d expect from something like this happening, or does it feel like a normal response to the stress of having your sister and the baby move back in?\n\nPatient: I mean, it feels like a pretty normal response to me. It's a big change, having a crying baby and my sister suddenly back in the house taking up so much space. I don't think my reaction is way out of proportion or anything. It's just... a really stressful situation to live in right now, so it makes sense to me that I'd be feeling kind of drained and a little behind on things.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your mood has been consistently low, even during times when you're not actively reminded of the situation at home, or does it only come up when you're directly dealing with or thinking about the stress at home?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's mostly just when I'm dealing with it or thinking about it. Like, if I'm at the shop working or if I'm out with friends, I usually feel pretty normal. It's really just when I'm at home and the baby is crying, or when I'm trying to study and I can't focus because of the noise. That's when I start feeling really stressed and down. When I'm away from the house, I can kind of forget about it for a while.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
